  1. Hidden Representation in Bicameralism: Petitions, Second Chambers, and Complementary Representation in the Japanese Diet
    Ken Kinoshita · pp. 1-30 · online 2026-10-30
    摘要

    This article examines petitions as channels of complementary representation under bicameralism. Complementary representation refers to cross-chamber differentiation in the institutional pathways through which citizens’ and groups’ demands are connected to parliament, formally recognized, and processed without necessarily producing policy change. Using 15,674 petitions submitted to the Japanese Diet between 2001 and 2025, the article compares petition adoption and functions in the House of Representatives (H.R.) and the House of Councillors (H.C.). Although raw adoption rates differ little, the full-period controlled model yields average predicted adoption probabilities of 5.9 percent for H.C. petitions and 3.2 percent for H.R. petitions. The negative H.R. coefficient remains significant in several full-period specifications, including a title-only analysis, but is smaller under title-only classification. The adoption difference is concentrated before the 210th Diet, while functional differences are sensitive to textual comparability. The findings extend bicameralism research beyond inter-chamber bargaining by showing that second chambers can provide distinct institutional pathways through which social demands become visible and formally recognized. They also identify the production and availability of parliamentary records as an important measurement condition for comparative research on representative functions.

  4. Altercasting China as a Partner: Vietnam and Cambodia's Divergent Foreign Policy Responses
    Vu Quy Son, Nguyen Cong Tung, Hoan Quang Truong · pp. 1-27 · online 2026-10-13
    摘要

    How do small states interpret and respond to China's rise? This article employs altercasting in role theory to compare Vietnam's and Cambodia's foreign policy responses towards China. It argues that despite both maintaining comprehensive strategic cooperative partnerships with China, the two smaller states have developed different China policies due to divergent expectations of China's roles. Rather than reflecting material asymmetry alone, this divergence stems from contrasting altercastings of China's behaviour under the same institutional framework. While both view China as an important cooperative partner, Vietnam frequently perceives China as failing to enact the role of a trusted partner, particularly in security and trade, where Chinese actions are seen as contradicting stated commitments. By contrast, Cambodia views China's behaviour as consistent with its expectations. The argument is developed through a paired comparison across two case studies: South China Sea disputes and the Belt and Road Initiative.

  6. When Do Chinese Workers’ Protests Survive? Conditions and Causes for the Backlash of Repression
    Yongsu Cho, Kyung Suk Lee · pp. 1-22 · online 2026-10-12
    摘要

    Under what conditions does state repression of workers’ protests backfire rather than suppress? Chinese authorities quell many labour protests swiftly, yet others persist and escalate despite coercion, and the sources of this variation remain unclear. Through process-tracing of four critical cases, the Yue Yuen Strike, the Jasic Incident, the Ansteel Lianzhong Protest, and the Quang Viet Protest, this article argues that repression outcomes are shaped before the state resorts to force. Two pre-repression conditions together generate what we term institutional cooptation failure: the absence of a channel binding workers to the All-China Federation of Trade Unions (ACFTU), and leadership by migrant workers. The two interlock, since migrant-led protests are rarely absorbed by a union whose organizational routines are anchored in locally registered workers. By relocating the determinants of protest outcomes from the aftermath of repression to the institutional terrain that precedes it, the study shows how cooptation failure can paradoxically sustain the mobilization of excluded workers and blunt repression under authoritarian rule.

  7. Accidental Conflict in the Taiwan Strait and on the Korean Peninsula: A Comparative Analysis
    Sungmin Cho · pp. 1-14 · online 2026-10-08
    摘要

    The potential for simultaneous conflicts in the Taiwan Strait and on the Korean Peninsula has emerged as one of the most critical security challenges in East Asia. While analysts have primarily focused on how North Korea might respond to a Taiwan contingency, less attention has been paid to the reverse scenario: how China might react in the Taiwan Strait if North Korea attacks South Korea. For a more balanced analysis, this article compares the structural and political variables shaping the likelihood of conflict in both regions. I argue that, beyond intentional attacks at the strategic level, we must also consider the unintentional consequences of low-level aggression at the tactical level. From this perspective, the Korean Peninsula presents a higher risk of accidental conflict and faster escalation than the Taiwan Strait. This study underscores not only the strategic linkage between the two regions but also the critical importance of conflict’s sequence in shaping regional stability.

  25. Chinese and Australian Aid in the Pacific: Which is More Effective at Developing Influence?
    Oliver Sinclair · pp. 1-13 · online 2026-09-24
    摘要

    In 2022, China and Solomon Islands signed a security deal allowing for the Chinese navy to potentially use Honiara’s ports. In that same year, Beijing attempted to author a region-wide security pact with ten Pacific Island Countries. These moves signalled a new strategic reality in the Pacific—China had arrived as a power with intent to shape international cooperation in the region. Those same moves reflected another reality—Australia’s place in the Pacific as the security partner of choice was now contested. This paper examines aid as a theatre of contest in the Pacific, and compares the approaches of China and Australia for developing influence in Pacific Island Countries. I argue that China’s focus on infrastructure has achieved significant soft power gains and resulted in regional alignment with Beijing’s strategic goals. I also suggest that Australia’s multi-modal aid practice is struggling to secure its strategic aims and a recalibration of Australian aid funding is needed to meet the needs of Pacific Island Countries. Using population surveys conducted throughout the Pacific, this paper reveals the extent of awareness and affability towards Chinese aid in the Pacific. The results contain timely lessons for Australia if it wishes to remain the region’s partner of choice.

  26. Gradual, Cumulative, Consequential: Monetization and the Rise of Private Domestic Demand in North Korea
    Ruediger Frank · pp. 1-21 · online 2026-09-22
    摘要

    North Korea's economy has undergone a fundamental transformation over the past three decades, yet its significance remains underappreciated. This article advances a monetization thesis: the restoration of money's core functions—not marketization per se—is the analytically decisive process driving that change. After the collapse of state distribution in the mid-1990s, money again served as medium of exchange, unit of account, and store of value, setting off a causal chain from private wealth accumulation to demand formation to structural pressure on the state. Drawing on development economics, Kornai's analysis of shortage economies, and a range of North Korean and third-country sources, the article traces the expansion of private consumption from basic necessities to higher-order goods and services. The channels through which private wealth might leave the country remain closed, so purchasing power accumulates at home, frustrating a middle class with limited opportunities to spend it and generating inflation that falls hardest on those who earn in won. The resulting pressure narrows the state's options to repression or selective liberalization. Should the latter prevail, North Korea would converge—unevenly and incompletely—toward a developmental dictatorship, rendering it more responsive to both sanctions and engagement.

The China Journal

Vol. 96 · 2026-07-01 · 37 篇 · U Chicago Press (ANU) · China · Peer-reviewed
  1. The Maoist Roots of China’s Extreme Income Inequality
    Martin King Whyte · pp. 1-27
    摘要

    China today has more extreme income inequality, according to some measures, than the United States, the most unequal advanced capitalist society. The conventional view is that when Mao Zedong was in charge, he relentlessly pursued greater social equality, even at the expense of China’s growth rate. However, a closer look at the contours and drivers of current inequalities tells a different story. The cardinal feature of income inequality in China today is a very large, and generally increasing, gap between the household incomes of rural origin citizens and their urban origin counterparts. China’s unusually large urban-rural income gap is rooted in, and perpetuated by, the system of household registration ( hukou ) that was institutionalized under Mao in 1958. By perpetuating the hukou -based division of Chinese into lower caste rural origin citizens and favored urban origin citizens, while relaxing the Mao-era restrictions on rural to urban migration, China in the reform era benefited by exploiting the undercompensated labor of millions of rural migrant laborers, arguably the major engine of the China boom. Despite some modest reforms of the hukou system in recent years, most of the more than 250 million rural–urban migrants in large cities today still face considerable obstacles and discrimination in trying to gain access to urban public goods and benefits for themselves and their children. But today the stubborn persistence of hukou -based discrimination poses major obstacles for China’s future. Three concrete obstacles are discussed: the low human capital levels of China’s adult population, the difficulty of shifting the economy to emphasize consumption over savings, and the declining number of babies being born each year. Each of these obstacles has roots in the hukou system institutionalized under Mao.

  2. Who Will Deliver Common Prosperity in China? A Look at Policy Instruments
    Jielu Yao, Christine Wong · pp. 28-63
    摘要

    This article examines how Xi Jinping’s common prosperity agenda has been translated into policy practice in China. Using the PKULaw database, we construct two original datasets: 194 common prosperity policies (CPPs), whose titles explicitly include “common prosperity,” and 10,180 common prosperity-linked policies (CPLPs), whose texts reference the term. Combining descriptive analysis, structural topic modeling, and dictionary-based keyword searches, we show that explicit CPP branding is limited and that it is highly concentrated in Zhejiang, the designated demonstration zone, with limited uptake elsewhere. Within the broader Xi-era CPLP corpus, the dominant themes are public service delivery and political signaling, while more ambitious redistributive, disciplinary, and regulatory domains are weakly represented or absent. These findings suggest that common prosperity in formal policy practice has been much narrower and more welfare administrative than its expansive political rhetoric implies, and they highlight the limits of a province-led approach to implementing an agenda that would require stronger central commitment and more substantial fiscal and regulatory support.

  3. The Rise of China’s Electric Vehicle Industry: Strategic Alliances Between Local Governments and Private Capital
    Fengming Lu, Xiao Ma · pp. 64-87
    摘要

    This article challenges the prevailing view that China’s electric vehicle (EV) boom is primarily the result of top-down industrial policy. Drawing on the history of China’s automotive sector since the 1990s, it shows that stringent central regulations and entrenched state-owned enterprises (SOEs) excluded most localities from lucrative joint ventures. In response, local governments forged strategic alliances with private manufacturers, leveraging capital markets, policy loopholes, and post-2008 credit expansion to attract investment, upgrade industries, and diversify regional economies. These partnerships, initially in traditional automotive manufacturing, proved pivotal to China’s EV takeoff after 2015, enabling private firms to outcompete SOEs in innovation, responsiveness, and market expansion. Comparative cases in steel and pharmaceuticals illustrate the broader applicability of this dynamic. The study highlights the enduring adaptability of Chinese local governments and private capital in navigating central constraints, offering new insights into industrial policies, industrial upgrading, and local developmental states.

  4. Historical Nihilism: The Rise of a Political Technology
    Jean Christopher Mittelstaedt · pp. 88-113
    摘要

    This article interrogates the Chinese Communist Party’s (CCP) evolving struggle over the past by tracing the career of the concept of historical nihilism (历史虚无主义), from polemic to a fully institutionalized yet diffuse political technology under Xi Jinping. Through close analysis of historical documents, Party resolutions, laws, disciplinary codes, court rulings, propaganda circulars, and digital governance directives, it identifies two phases: a pre-2013 rhetorical stage, during which the term remained discursively unstable and primarily served to stigmatize heterodox scholarship, and a post-2013 institutional stage, in which it was recast as an existential threat and embedded across four domains of Party-state power: law, discipline, education, and cyberspace. To account for this transformation, the article draws on Althusser’s concept of interpellation, along with Bourdieu and Butler’s insights on performative authority, to show how historical nihilism evolved from a polemical label into a political technology capable of triggering juridical, bureaucratic, and algorithmic sanctions. This shift exemplifies the CCP’s transition from narrating history to governing its possibility, and from rhetorical contestation to epistemic sovereignty: the sovereign power to determine what history is, who may speak it, and how it must be remembered.

  5. Urban Investment and Development Companies in China’s Local Governance: The Paradox of Marketization
    Zhiyu Wang · pp. 114-140
    摘要

    As financing platforms and construction agents for local governments, urban investment and development companies (chengshi touzi fazhan gongsi 城市投资发展公司), or UIDCs, play a crucial role in China’s urbanization and local governance. Existing research has often treated UIDCs as static instruments of local governments while overlooking their evolving, and at times conflicting, objectives and their complex governance roles within local administrations. Drawing on 15 months of fieldwork within a district government and its affiliated UIDCs, this article examines UIDCs within the broader dynamics of state capitalism and central-local relations. First, it argues that despite marketization reforms promoting corporate governance and substantive business operations, UIDCs remain deeply embedded in local governance structures, particularly in less developed regions, where they continue to serve as key vehicles for local governments’ developmental and fiscal objectives. Second, rather than clarifying the boundary between the state and the market as officially intended, marketization has further intensified tensions between the two. To navigate both the central government’s reform agenda and local political imperatives, UIDCs often adopt adaptive strategies to reconcile multiple and conflicting pressures. These practices offer short-term remedies, while also introducing unintended financial and governance risks. By examining the paradoxical outcomes of UIDCs’ marketization at the local level, this article contributes to a more nuanced understanding of how state and market interact amid central-local tensions in contemporary China.

  1. Social Work on the China-Myanmar Border: State-NGO Synchronization
    You Tianlong, Tian Yiran · pp. 000-000 · online 2026-08-18
    摘要

    This article investigates the evolving relationship between the Chinese state and nongovernmental organizations (NGOs) through the case of Ruili, a county-level city on the China-Myanmar frontier. Drawing on ethnographic fieldwork, interviews, and extensive archival records, we develop an analytical framework of synchronization for the Chinese state and NGOs to capture how the relationship between them has changed. The analysis identifies four stages: a compensatory phase, in which foreign NGOs filled governance gaps and nurtured local capacity; a parallel growth phase, in which domestic NGOs and local governments expanded activities together; an alignment phase, in which NGOs reoriented themselves to implement national strategies such as rural revitalization and public health security; and a global outreach phase, in which selected NGOs extended their services abroad within the Belt and Road Initiative (BRI). We present a case in which a welfare NGO working on cross-border social problems developed its operations alongside local state agencies. This article contributes to current debates on the adaptation of Chinese NGOs to a new environment by showing how coproduction, strategic alignment, and bounded negotiation have unfolded over time in a politically sensitive frontier setting.

The China Quarterly

Vol. 266 · 2026-06 · 38 篇 · Cambridge UP (SOAS) · China · Peer-reviewed
  1. CQY volume 266 Cover and Back matter
    pp. b1-b2 · online 2026-08-03

    pp. f1-f6 · online 2026-08-03
  3. The Resilience of Self-reliance in China: Autonomy, Interdependence and Order-shaping
    Amy King, Wenting He · pp. 291-308 · online 2026-05-20
    摘要

    The idea of “self-reliance” has endured in Chinese political discourse for nearly a century, transcending profound changes in China’s political, economic and strategic circumstances. Although it is frequently misinterpreted as economic isolation or autarky, the idea of self-reliance in China has always acknowledged the country’s engagement with the global economy. Drawing on the discursive institutionalist concept of ideational resilience, we show that self-reliance comprises three interlocking elements: autonomy, interdependence and order-shaping. While these sit in tension with one another, they have also accommodated one another since the earliest articulations of the idea. This tripartite structure has enabled Chinese leaders since the Republican era to reinterpret and usefully deploy the idea of self-reliance. Our findings underscore the resilience of Chinese foreign economic policy ideas, as well as the ideational logic behind Xi Jinping’s seemingly contradictory pursuit of technological self-reliance, open global markets and greater connectivity with the developing world.

  4. Ideology Without Unity: The Rhizomatic Logic of Xi Jinping Thought
    Jean Christopher Mittelstaedt · pp. 309-324 · online 2026-05-11
    摘要

    This article reconceptualizes Xi Jinping Thought on Socialism with Chinese Characteristics (Xi Thought hereafter) not as a coherent doctrine but as a rhizomatic ideological formation. Drawing on the work of Gilles Deleuze and Félix Guattari, it treats Xi Thought as a configuration in which coherence is not presupposed but produced through the continual recombining of fragments across institutional sites. Based on an analysis of official publications, including collected works, excerpt volumes and study readers, the article shows how Xi’s speeches are disassembled and reassembled across domains such as law, economy, diplomacy and culture. These recompositions render ideology modular and resilient, allowing elements to be activated, re-weighted or sidelined without destabilizing the system as a whole. The article further argues that ideological coherence is generated through distributed and compulsory participation by Party and state actors, who are required to embed fragments of Xi Thought into institutional practice. Conceptualizing Xi Thought as a rhizome shifts analysis from doctrinal meaning to the operational logic through which ideology acquires administrative force within the Chinese party-state.

  5. Bureaucratic Restructuring and the State-led (De-)Financialization of Local Governance in China
    Xuchen Zhang, Fenghua Pan · pp. 325-344 · online 2026-06-04
    摘要

    State-led financialization has emerged as a salient feature of local governance in China, yet it remains underexplored through the lens of bureaucratic restructuring. This paper traces the evolution of local financial agencies since 2002 through four distinct stages: local financial offices ( jinrongban ), financial bureaus ( jinrongju ), financial regulatory bureaus ( jinrong jianguanju ) and financial commissions ( jinrongwei ). Initially, local governments established jinrongban to harness financial capital for regional development. By proactively connecting local firms with capital markets to facilitate corporate listings, they embedded financial logic into local governance, reflecting local state-led financialization. Subsequently, the central government formalized these bodies into jinrongju with expanded mandates, deepening state-led financialization. Later, it systematically devolved regulatory responsibilities and renamed them jinrong jianguanju , elevating regulatory functions to a top priority, illustrating how the central government selectively steered the direction of financialization. Ultimately, provincial agencies were reorganized into jinrongwei and stripped of development functions, while city-level counterparts were largely closed, signifying a shift towards central state-led de-financialization. This trajectory was driven by shifting central–local dynamics, as early bottom-up initiatives were superseded by top-down control. The bureaucratic restructuring examined in this study offers new insights into the state-led (de-)financialization of local governance in China.

  6. Explaining China’s High Welfare Spending: Local Models and Drivers of Fiscally Oriented Affordable Housing
    Ge Xin, Hanyu Chen · pp. 345-364 · online 2026-03-24
    摘要

    While the world has been undergoing a global wave of welfare retrenchment since the turn of the 21st century, China’s spending on welfare provision has grown remarkably. Through a comparative study of the public housing provision programmes in two districts within the same municipality, this paper explores models of fiscally oriented public housing provision and their underlying driving forces. Drawing on data from archives, interviews and intensive fieldwork conducted in south-west China from 2014 to 2018, we identify two prominent public housing models: the entrepreneurial model and the extractive model. We propose that the choice of different welfare provision approaches is linked to local fiscal pressures and the target responsibility system. This paper sheds light on the structure of political incentives within China’s local governments in relation to public service provision and explores how local leaders improve public welfare in a non-Western setting.

  7. Governing through Visibility: Local Government Experimentation with Internet Celebrity Cities in China
    Lu Liu, Yahua Wang · pp. 365-384 · online 2026-04-15
    摘要

    The rise of internet celebrity cities has become one of the most striking phenomena in China since 2021. How do local governments respond and harness this trend to advance their development goals? This study focuses on local experimentation in creating such cities, drawing on the case of the Village Football Super League ( Cun chao 村超) in Guizhou. We identify policy entrepreneurship as a key driver of local experimentation and highlight three core strategies for creating internet celebrity cities: crafting local symbols, co-producing viral content and mitigating public opinion risk. Further analysis shows that this experimentation, by attracting massive public attention, simultaneously promotes economic growth, strengthens social cohesion, reinforces state narratives and projects China’s national image onto the global stage. Overall, the findings suggest an emerging model in which public attention becomes a core resource for local development and governance in China’s digital era.

  8. From Contention to Clientelism: Evidence from the Ten-Year Fishing Ban in China
    Jingping Liu · pp. 385-399 · online 2026-04-13
    摘要

    How do ordinary Chinese people circumvent unpopular state policies? The existing literature primarily focuses on resistance against local bureaucrats. Drawing on ethnographic research on the ten-year fishing ban in the Yangtze River Basin, this article finds that fishermen (clients) continue to fish by maintaining patron–client relationships with the enforcers of the fishing ban (patrons). Ordinary fishermen seek the protection of enforcers through bribery. Enhanced state monitoring under the fishing ban facilitates bribery-based clientelism by weakening the fishermen’s everyday resistance, but it also constrains the power of enforcers by increasing the risk that their corruption will be discovered by upper-level authorities. For extremely poor fishermen, who are barely able to afford to pay bribes, their daily acts of resistance are morally justified by the need for subsistence safety, presenting enforcers with a dilemma: they must fulfil their law enforcement duties while also ensuring the survival of these individuals to maintain social stability. Therefore, cultivating a clientelist relationship with impoverished fishermen enables enforcers to manage their noncompliance, thereby balancing these conflicting goals. While clientelism protects people from unpopular policies to some extent, it more fundamentally strengthens the power of local bureaucrats, creating the potential for greater exploitation and larger-scale popular grievances in the long run.

  9. Promoting Meritocracy through Recognition: The Post-recognition Career Trajectories of China’s Model County Party Secretaries
    Weilin Xiao, Haoyue Hu, Cai (Vera) Zuo · pp. 400-417 · online 2026-06-01
    摘要

    The increasingly proactive role of central authority in managing subprovincial leaders is understudied and our understanding of the function of political recognition in contemporary China remains inadequate. By adopting an expanded view of political meritocracy and using difference-in-differences analysis of original data about China’s model county Party secretaries, we demonstrate the ramifications of political recognition in generating a merit-based fast track and find that the recognition of model county Party secretaries has a lasting positive impact on recipients’ subsequent career advancement, although this impact is uneven. These findings enhance our understanding of the role of centrally bestowed political honours in promoting meritocracy, the non-institutionalized soft measures in shaping political incentives and the central–local interaction in the dynamics of political recognition in contemporary China.

  10. Solving Agency Problems in Chinese Universities: Academics in the Shadow of the Party’s Declared Leadership
    Alexandra Kaiser · pp. 418-434 · online 2026-04-15
    摘要

    How does the party-state exercise leadership over universities and manage the individuals embedded in the university system without restraining their capacity for excellence and innovation? I argue that the presidential responsibility system has resolved a fundamental agency problem in Chinese universities. The system is supported by a set of mechanisms designed to enforce loyalty to the leadership of the Chinese Communist Party. It can easily adapt to political changes and thus maintain authoritarian rule without compromising the overarching agenda of research excellence.

  11. Political Satire and the Decline of Discourse Quality: Evidence from China’s Online Public Sphere
    Xiaotong Chen, Tianru Guan · pp. 435-452 · online 2026-03-30
    摘要

    Extensive research has examined whether political satire promotes or suppresses political discussion, but far less attention has been paid to how it shapes discourse quality. This study investigates the impact of political satire on discourse quality by scrutinizing the substance of online discussions in a non-democratic context. Drawing on manual content analysis of discussions on a major Chinese social media platform surrounding China’s controversial policy to raise the age of retirement, we analysed original posts and subsequent replies (N = 3,400) to assess the impact of satire on online political talk. The results show that political satire significantly decreases topic relevance and rationality, while exerting no significant effect on civility, and virtually precludes argument reciprocity. These findings contribute theoretically by shifting the focus from whether satire stimulates political engagement to how it influences the substantive quality of deliberation. They also invite a reassessment of the often-overemphasized optimistic role of political satire, highlighting its potential to erode rational and constructive discourse.

  12. Desperate Acts of Democracy: Identity Threat, Autocratization and Voter Turnout in Hong Kong
    Lev Nachman, Nathan Kar Ming Chan, Chit Wai John Mok · pp. 453-469 · online 2026-06-03
    摘要

    How does identity threat affect mass political participation during times of autocratization? Building on social identity and group threat literatures, we theorize that perceived threats to Hong Konger identity increase voter turnout; however, the political salience of this threat is contingent on the degree of autocratization. We test this argument utilizing the case of Hong Kong, whose ongoing incorporation into the People’s Republic of China (PRC) has entered an unprecedented era of autocratization. We argue that threats to Hong Konger identity came to mobilize voter participation, especially as autocratization progressed in Hong Kong. Hong Kongers resisted during this period of political change through their intention to turn out to vote. We conducted three original experiments during different transitionary periods of Hong Kong’s autocratic shift. We find that priming identity threat had no effect on voter turnout before Hong Kong’s autocratic divergence. However, once more extreme forms of autocratization began, Hong Kongers assigned to an identical identity threat treatment were substantially more likely to report a higher likelihood of voting. This mobilizing effect persisted in a third experiment. Our results suggest a paradox where citizens’ propensity to vote increases when faced with perceived shrinking opportunities to participate in elections during times of authoritarian transition.

  13. China’s Internal Security Spending: An Assessment of New Data
    Sheena Chestnut Greitens · pp. 470-480 · online 2026-03-26
    摘要

    Internal security has been a governance priority under Xi Jinping. How does China’s budget reflect this prioritization? This research report presents updated data on China’s internal security spending, 1992–2022, revealing a mix of continuity and change. Domestic security expenditure continues to rise, more than doubling from 2012 to 2022, but has risen mostly in proportion to the People’s Republic of China’s overall expenditure. The balance between central and local expenditure has shifted further towards local spending, which, in the context of rising local fiscal constraint, may increase pressure on local public security bureaus. The Ministry of Public Security continues to receive the largest share of domestic security spending, while the proportion of internal security spending allocated to the People’s Armed Police (PAP) has decreased, probably reflecting the reorganization of the PAP in 2017–2018. Spending per capita and relative to GDP continues to be higher in locations that are politically sensitive, including Beijing, Tibet and Xinjiang.

  14. Discovering Controversies through Reversions: A Text-based Analysis of China’s Two-phase Bill Changes
    Jiying Jiang · pp. 481-490 · online 2026-05-11
    摘要

    This study uncovers hidden disputes in China’s law-making process by systematically tracking bill changes across executive and legislative phases. Utilizing an original dataset of 45 executive-initiated bills (2008–2023), it identifies a consistent pattern of reversions – instances where executive-approved changes to draft bills were overridden in the legislature – revealing the National People’s Congress to be a key policy battleground. Reversions are concentrated in bills concerning health, safety and environment, often involving scope, regulatory frameworks and legal liability. Combined with qualitative case studies, these findings demonstrate the legislature’s crucial role in facilitating monitoring and negotiation in the policy process, offering new insights into executive–legislative dynamics in China’s single-party regime.

  15. How the Chinese Public Perceives South-East Asia: Affinity, Annoyance and Apprehension
    Enze Han · pp. 491-505 · online 2026-05-29
    摘要

    This article explores how the Chinese public perceives South-East Asia. Using original survey data collected in China, it suggests three perceptual frames. The “affinity” frame, which is rooted in historical Chinese migration and diaspora ties, generates feelings of kinship and cultural connection. Conversely, the “annoyance” frame stems from contemporary geopolitical tensions, particularly South China Sea disputes, where several South-East Asian countries are viewed as provocateurs challenging China’s regional authority. The “apprehension” frame characterizes the region as exotic yet dangerous, a perception reinforced by recent cyber scams and criminal activities targeting Chinese citizens, creating stereotypes of South-East Asia as lawless and unsafe. The article analyses the survey data for the statistical distribution of these frames and discusses their political implications. By exploring how historical ties, geopolitical tensions and security concerns simultaneously shape Chinese public opinion, the article illuminates the complex perceptual landscape that may influence China’s diplomatic and economic relations with South-East Asia.

  16. Forever Hong Kong: A Global City’s Decolonization Struggle Ching Kwan Lee. Cambridge, MA: Harvard University Press, 2025. 344 pp. £37.95 (hbk). ISBN 9780674290198
    Samson Yuen · pp. 506-508 · online 2026-06-01
    摘要

    Forever Hong Kong offers a bold reinterpretation of the city's contentious political trajectory from the handover era to the present.The book is masterfully written and meticulously researched.It is a unique and valuable contribution to Hong Kong studies and beyond.Lee's core question is how Hong Kongers transformed from "colonized subjects" complacent with the status quo into "decolonizing subjects" willing to take action and chart their own history.Her answer is that Hong Kong's successive waves of contention -from the 2003 Article 23 protests through the 2014 Umbrella Movement to the 2019 Anti-Extradition movement -are best understood as a decolonization struggle, rather than a conventional fight for democratization.The colonialism to be unravelled, she argues, was not purely British.It was a "double coloniality" co-produced by London and Beijing, held together by four interlocking "colonial myths" that constrained Hong Kongers' political agency and imagination: stability and prosperity, free-market capitalism, rule of law, and China as destiny.The first chapter reconstructs how each pillar was manufactured and hardened into common sense; the rest of the book traces how various political and economic ruptures pushed activists and ordinary citizens to challenge each pillar in turn, culminating in the 2019 uprising that Lee calls both "the peak of the city's ongoing decolonization struggle decades in the making and the opening salvo of a new era of confrontation between Western neoliberal capitalism and Chinese state capitalism" (p.2).The book's empirical depth is impressive.Lee draws on ethnographic fieldwork from 2019 through 2023, in-depth interviews with a wide cross-section of participants -from frontline militants and student leaders to bankers, medics, accountants and diaspora organizers -as well as archival materials and intellectual writings.Chapters four and five provide particularly illuminating details about militant frontline protesters and the international front, which had remained obscure to outsiders.By recasting Hong Kong's political history as a quest for decolonization, Lee offers a refreshing perspective.Most scholarship on Hong Kong's politics -including much of my own -works within contentious politics or democratization theory, privileging topics such as hybrid regimes, electoral authoritarianism, opportunity structures and repertoire shifts.These works focus on both structure and dynamics: what explains protest participation, how movements escalate and how political opportunities open and close.Lee poses a different question: how did people come to understand what they were doing, and why?The payoff is most visible in her analysis of protest violence.While contentious politics scholars tend to explain radicalization through state repression and organizational dynamics, Lee reconstructs how "brave warriors" themselves theorized and negotiated the use of violence.These accounts capture emotional, ethical and calculative dimensions that analyses on structure and dynamics tend to flatten.

  3. Managing Hydro-dependence and Risk: Hong Kong’s Water Insurance Policy, 1967–1982
    Florence Mok · pp. 1-19 · online 2026-10-01
    摘要

    Using archival and unofficial sources, this article examines how political calculations, economic considerations, local consumption patterns and international dynamics shaped the colonial administration’s water governance strategies in Hong Kong. It focuses on how the 1967 water emergency prompted the colonial government to recalibrate its approach, from a policy of manageable dependency to one of risk aversion. After 1967, an “insurance policy” ensuring a “basic ration” was pursued. This led to the construction of the Lok On Pai desalination plant. The article then traces developments from 1976 to 1982, when Hong Kong gradually moved to a regionally integrated strategy, driven not only by improved Sino-British relations but also by economic considerations, growing public intolerance of water rationing and the Middle East oil crisis. By 1982, Hong Kong’s water insurance policy had officially ended; Chinese water was classified as “reliable.” By exploring these understudied developments and factors, the article contributes to the histories of water management, colonial Hong Kong and China.

  19. Balancing Accommodation and Authority: Husbands’ Family Strategies in Transnational Marriages in North-East China
    Yuanyuan Hu, Zhonglu Li · pp. 1-17 · online 2026-09-07
    摘要

    This article examines South–South transnational marriages between Vietnamese women and Chinese men in North-East China, focusing on the strategies husbands’ families employ to sustain these unions. Drawing on Bourdieu’s theory of family strategies and in-depth interviews with 26 households, it develops a “departure-restraint” framework to explain how receiving families respond to the persistent possibility of a bride’s marital exit. The findings show that these strategies rest on a balance between limited accommodation and selective authority: Vietnamese brides are granted a degree of autonomy in everyday matters, while husbands’ families retain control over major household decisions. The article further argues that the relatively narrow economic gap between sending and receiving regions in South–South marriages reshapes household dynamics in distinctive ways. Under conditions of limited economic opportunity, husbands’ families maintain marital stability not through outright domination, but through calibrated negotiations over everyday autonomy and household authority.

  20. Feminized Revival: Han Chinese Dress, Gender and Cultural Nationalism in Contemporary China
    Fuyun Wei · pp. 1-17 · online 2026-09-01
    摘要

    Women hold disproportionate authority in China’s contemporary Hanfu revival, where Han-ness operates through implicit cultural codes rather than explicit ethnic claims. Recent scholarship highlights how Han-ness circulates as a moral, etiquette-centred grammar of “proper culture.” Drawing on 18 months of ethnography across six sites in China, this article shows how gender mediates that grammar and shapes the performances of Han-ness. Women, as arbiters of taste and propriety, enact an assertive banal nationalism through aesthetic and organizational labour. Men, marginal within the field, perform defensive overt nationalism through didactic historical narratives and moralized claims to legitimacy. To explain this pattern of identity formation and boundary making, I advance the notion of gendered everyday nationhood. The article contributes to scholarship on nationalism by showing how embodied practices sustain national belonging, and how mundane evaluation and boundary policing reproduce nationhood in everyday life.

  21. The Origins of Land Finance: Local Resistance and Top-Down Imposition
    Saul Kriger Wilson · pp. 1-20 · online 2026-09-01
    摘要

    Land sales have been a significant source of revenue for Chinese local governments since the early 2000s. Contrary to popular belief, local governments did not turn to land finance to make up for lost tax revenues after the 1994 tax sharing reform. Rather, both before and after 1994, corrupt land sales were widespread, as officials sold land far below market prices in return for bribes. Even as the fiscal incentives for local governments to adopt land finance increased in the 1990s, state agents continued acting on their own personal interests rather than the institutional interests of local governments. Local governments only began to sell land at higher, market prices when an anti-corruption campaign launched by the central government made doing so a political imperative in the early 2000s. Local governments were much more responsive to political imperatives than to fiscal incentives.

  22. Subversive Amplification: Online Dissent and the Limits of Information Control in China
    Christian Göbel · pp. 1-18 · online 2026-08-28
    摘要

    Intensified information control severely constrains digital contention in China, yet online mass events occasionally occur. Using a dataset of 274,721 Weibo messages, this study examines mobilization, coordination and grievance articulation during a two-day episode of online dissent in the aftermath of the 2022 Urumqi residential building fire, which preceded the White Paper protests. It argues that users were activated by public communication failures that transformed manifold grievances into outrage, and that the associated hashtags provided anchors to which criticism could be attached. In a process this study terms “subversive amplification,” concentrated engagement amplified the hashtags’ visibility, and with it the critical content attached to them. However, absent the unifying function of opinion leaders, grievance articulation remained fragmented and did not converge on unified demands. The case illustrates the inherent vulnerabilities of information control but also the conditions and limits of their exploitation.

  23. Rising Engagement and Substantive Responsiveness: 13 Years of Online Petitioning in China
    Matt DeButts, Tongtong Zhang · pp. 1-17 · online 2026-08-05
    摘要

    The Chinese government has invested heavily in online petition platforms to channel public grievances, yet systematic longitudinal evidence on citizen–state interactions within these venues remains limited. This research report presents nationwide evidence showing that both public participation and government responsiveness through e-petition institutions have increased markedly since Xi Jinping took office. Analysing more than four million citizen appeals submitted to the country’s largest online petition portal, Message Board for Leaders, from January 2011 to January 2024, we find a twelvefold rise in petition volume and a fivefold increase in users, largely driven by urban residents in eastern provinces. Appeals have also diversified, with notable growth in housing-related issues and more strategic appeals directed at Party secretaries. Meanwhile, local governments have raised response rates from 50 per cent to 98 per cent, shortened average wait times from 176 to 19 days, and increased the substantive resolution rate from 17 per cent to 31 per cent over the past decade.

  4. Toward Divergence: Social Trends in Tomb Epitaphs During Liao, Song, and Jin China
    Ya-Hwei Hsu 許雅惠 · pp. 1-42
    摘要

    This study presents a comprehensive view of middle-period China by focusing on excavated epitaphs from Liao, Song, and Jin tombs. It systematically examines the social backgrounds of epitaph users, compares their compositions across dynasties, and delineates societal trends in North and South China. Although the stratum of officials dominated the use of epitaphs across all dynasties, the overall social composition of epitaph users reveals divergences between northern and southern dynasties. The Liao displayed the strongest aristocratic traits, with a remarkable proportion of epitaphs belonging to upper-level Khitan nobles. The Northern Song exhibited meritocratic characteristics, with a considerable number of epitaphs belonging to scholar-officials and shi 士 literati. It also featured the most diverse backgrounds among non-literati elite users, some of whom were expressive in their epitaphs, suggesting a lively local society. The destructive wars of the early twelfth century deepened the North-South divide. In the North, under the Jin, Jurchen and Han Chinese elites turned away from the use of epitaphs. In the Southern Song, the literati predominated among burials with epitaphs, while non-literati elites became more silent compared to their Northern Song predecessors. Many new types of epitaphs emerged in the southeastern region, where Neo-Confucianism flourished. These may have served as a means for Neo-Confucians to express their intellectual identity. The variety of these epitaphs further signaled growing internal differentiation among literati users. This macro-level comparative research of excavated epitaphs illuminates the distinctive characteristics of each society, traces long-term developments, and offers explanations for these changes. 摘要: 南北對峙的遼、宋、金是中古向近世中國轉變的關鍵時期。本研究全 面收集遼、宋、金出土墓誌，以統計方法分析其使用者，宏觀呈現各朝上層 菁英在「死後世界」的組成與差異。本文發現，各朝墓誌的主要使用者都是官 吏，但整體的使用者身份組成有明顯南、北差異。遼代集中於契丹貴族，北 宋多為地方菁英。南、北差異隨宋金戰爭而擴大。金代隨葬墓誌的上層菁英遽 減，女真貴族亦罕用墓誌。南宋墓誌的隨葬增加，且更集中在官吏與士人， 東南地區亦出現許多新型態的墓誌，推測與理學興起有關。從跨越朝代與 國別的出土墓誌可見南、北社會的特點與發展。

  5. Slavery and Zhang Dai’s Aesthetics
    Tina Lu 呂立亭 · pp. 43-70
    摘要

    This article argues that the celebrated aesthetics of Tao’an mengyi 陶庵夢憶 by Zhang Dai 張岱 (1597–1689) are inseparable from the system of bonded labor that structured late-imperial Chinese elite culture. Often read as exemplary memory writing, the collection constructs an aesthetic centered on spectacle, surface, and perceptual disorientation. I argue that this aesthetic both depends on and obscures a pervasive system of human commodification, which I approach through the analytic lens of slavery. Drawing on recent scholarship that emphasizes the ubiquity of trafficking, the article shifts focus from defining enslaved populations to examining a broader system of social transactionality. Readings of essays on theater, visual display, and elite sociability show how aesthetic effects rely on the precise coordination—and erasure—of labor. Zhang Dai’s fascination with illusion and perfect staging reflects and reproduces a world structured by mastery over others. At moments, especially in depictions of sex work, this aesthetic distance becomes unstable, revealing underlying tensions. Ultimately, Tao’an mengyi not only represents but enacts an aesthetics of control, inviting readers to participate in transforming domination into aesthetic pleasure. 摘要: 本文主張，張岱之《陶庵夢憶》的美學與晚期帝制中國之依附性勞動體 制密不可分。其審美以奇觀、表面與感知錯置為核心，既奠基於亦遮蔽人身 商品化，本文以「奴隸制」作為分析概念。透過細讀戲曲、展示與社交場景，指 出審美效果依賴對勞動的精密調度並將其抹除，從而再生產支配他人的社 會結構。部分涉及性交易之描寫亦顯露出內在張力。總之，《陶庵夢憶》不 僅再現，亦實踐一種將支配轉化為審美愉悅的控制美學。

  6. Learning to Hear: Evidential Scholarship and the Ancient Song in High Qing China
    Matthew Wild 韋邁 · pp. 71-108
    摘要

    This article examines the power of song within the High Qing phil-ological movement known as evidential scholarship ( kaozheng xue 考證學). Focusing on the celebrated poet Huang Jingren 黃景仁 (1749–1783) and his association with the influential evidential scholar Zhu Yun 朱筠 (1729–1781) during Zhu’s tenure as education commissioner of Anhui Province, it challenges modern accounts that oppose lyric subjectivity to philological inquiry. I argue that prevailing understandings of both High Qing literature and scholarship fail to apprehend the integral connection between poetic and evidentiary practices in the period. For Qing writers such as Huang and Zhu, the idea of song afforded not merely aesthetic expression but a medium of self-evident, apodictic proof. Close readings of key episodes show how this claim to truth functioned as a nexus of shared immanence wherein writing presents itself as singing and reading unfolds as an act of present listening. By attending to this regime of “notional song,” the study reframes evidential scholarship not as a protoscientific divergence from the poetic but as a culture in which sound, citation, and proof were mutually constitutive. 摘要: 本文探討「歌」在清代盛世考證學語境中的作用。以詩人黃景仁 (1749–1783)及其與朱筠(1729–1781)在安徽學政任內的交往為線索，本 文重新審視近 現代將抒情的內在性對立與考證學術的敘述框架。作者指出，廣為流傳的 對清代文學與學術實踐的論述，往往忽略了詩歌作與考證學之間的內在聯 繫。對黃景仁與朱筠等人而言，「歌」不僅用於審美表達,更發揮著一種自證 自明的媒介作用。本文透過對「歌」的若干關鍵場景的細讀來說明，詩歌的 真理主張是在一種共享的臨在經驗中展開的——書寫在此呈現為歌唱，閱 讀則成為當下的聆聽。從「意念之歌」的角度出發，本文主張考證學不應被 理解為與詩性深具分歧的前科學，而應被納入一種能使聲音、引證與證成彼 此交織、相互生成的文化框架之中。

  3. Becoming Chinese American: The Chin Siblings During China’s War of Resistance
    Jin Feng · pp. 205-232
    摘要

    The Chin siblings, three second-generation Chinese Americans, went to their ancestral land to teach at institutions of higher education during China’s War of Resistance against Japanese Invasion (1931–1945). While there, they availed themselves of opportunities that were denied to Chinese Americans in the U.S. but also faced prejudice and suspicion. Nonetheless, in family and business correspondence they often portrayed themselves as patriotic citizens of both countries and cultural ambassadors who connected the two peoples. They employed such efforts to negotiate competing nationalist demands made by the Chinese and U.S. governments and thus offered invaluable insights into the complex processes of becoming Chinese American. This article shines a light on a significant yet little known body of materials to demonstrate how family dynamics, as well as political, racial, and cultural forces, shaped the identify formation of Chinese Americans in the mid-twentieth century, especially immediately preceding, during, and after World War II. Ultimately, it builds up the archives of Asian American history and amplifies Asian American voices from the unique perspective of a sibling unit’s wartime experiences.

  4. Making the AAPI Perspectives Oral History Project: Remembering a Place-Based Social History of Activism in Santa Clara Valley
    Yvonne Y. Kwan · pp. 233-270
    摘要

    This article examines the AAPI Perspectives: Oral History and Activism Digital Archive and Curriculum Project (2021–2024), which redirected county funding from carceral “hate crime” initiatives to community-based oral history. The article proceeds in three parts: first, it contextualizes the project’s genesis in the early 2020s; second, it analyzes the project’s oral history interviews to show how these AAPI social histories took actual place in Santa Clara Valley (1840s–present); and third, it argues that these overlapping regional histories facilitated an intergenerational, multiethnic spatial politics that catalyzed various activisms, which seeded social movements, advanced Asian American studies, and challenged the model minority myth. Together, these analyses highlight the potential of grassroots organizing and oral history to generate community-centered narratives that echo the founding vision of Asian American studies as a field committed to “serving the people.” AAPI Perspectives reframes AAPI pasts while modeling how communities can reclaim, preserve, and mobilize their histories in the pursuit of justice.

  5. (De)Tracking Sinocentrism: Mathematics Education + Racialized Electoral Politics in San Francisco
    Kyle L. Chong (張陳創庭), Sheila M. Orr · pp. 271-309
    摘要

    In 2024, San Francisco voters approved Proposition G, requiring 8th-grade Algebra 1 for public school students and restores “tracked” course sequences that sort students by their perceived intellectual abilities. Proposition G’s proponents mobilized anti-Asian American racist essentialism, and proximity to whiteness, targeting San Francisco’s large Chinese American electorate. We argue, using Asian Critical Race Theory and critical race discourse analysis, that Proposition G proponents’ arguments mobilized covert anti-Asian American essentialism and Sinocentrism to undermine racial justice discourses in educational politics. We consequently propose an analytic of “(De) Tracking Sinocentrism,” which disentangles Sinocentrism from Asian American panethnic racialization processes.

  6. A Pedagogical Guide to Tessa Hulls’s Feeding Ghosts : A Graphic Memoir
    Lan Dong, Monica Chiu · pp. 311-325

Modern Chinese Literature and Culture

Vol. 38, No. 1 · 2026-06 · 8 篇 · Edinburgh UP · Chinese literature & culture · Peer-reviewed
  1. Note from the Editors
    Natascha Gentz, Christopher Rosenmeier · pp. v-vi
    摘要

    Several articles in this issue of Modern Chinese Literature and Culture examine physical disability and the boundaries of body and mind.As usual, the various studies cover a wide range of approaches and sources, but to the extent that a common thread runs through many of these pieces, it relates to fragmentation.Bodies, minds, texts, and identities are divided, split, and, at least occasionally, reassembled or reconstituted through technological means.Deleuze and Guattari feature in three studies, all in different ways, showing the breadth of their theoretical frameworks and their wide applicability.Jiahe Mei analyzes Guo Moruo's 1928 fairy tale "One Hand," examining how disability serves as a constituent of revolutionary literature.By linking the short story's industrial injuries to Guo's own hearing loss, the author explores ironic temporalities and the deaf phonograph metaphor to redefine revolutionary subjectivity.Physical disability is configured in a dramatically different way in 1960s-1970s Hong Kong popular culture, as shown in Liu Jinping's examination of Hong Kong martial arts films and science fiction.Liu uses the "dividual" concept to view the broken body as a site for technological assemblage.This dividual body allowed creators to negotiate the socio-historical pressures of war, industrialization, and postcolonial identity through a fluid, non-essentialist sense of self.Moving on to the fragmentation of the mind, Richard Letteri analyzes Zhang Yimou's celebrated 1991 film Raise the Red Lantern

  2. “One Hand” and the Deaf Ear: Disabled Futures in Guo Moruo’s Revolutionary Fairy Tale
    Jiahe Mei · pp. 1-29
    摘要

    This essay revisits the agenda of “revolutionary literature” through the lens of “disability,” focusing on two overlooked aspects of Guo Moruo’s life: his 1928 fairy tale “One Hand” (Yi zhi shou), which thematizes workplace injuries as a catalyst for proletarian revolution, and his own hearing loss that spurred his transition from medicine to literature. By analyzing the tale’s ambivalent portrayal of disabled characters alongside the revolutionary writer’s sensorial and experiential loss amid a revolutionary crisis, I reveal how the fairy tale destabilizes normative assumptions about temporality and communication in revolutionary literature. In particular, I argue that the tale features an “ironic” temporality that precludes disabled aspirants from their envisioned futures as well as a “loud” pedagogy that displaces conceptions of a transparent voice with a materialist, processual notion of sound. Foregrounding the twin paradoxes that revolutionary triumph was envisioned in the wake of actual failure and that the well-known phonograph metaphor was articulated when Guo was nearly deaf, this essay complicates the debates on revolutionary subjectivity and paves new paths for conceptualizing disability not as an exception to but as a constituent of revolutionary literature and revolutionary futures.

  3. More than Dismemberment: The Fragmented Body in 1960s–1970s Hong Kong Popular Culture
    Liu Jinping · pp. 30-59
    摘要

    Bodily fragmentation emerged as a significant topos in Hong Kong popular culture in the 1960s and 1970s, as it was produced, circulated, and proliferated across interconnected genres, such as martial arts novels, science fiction, and action films. Existing studies, informed by the notion of a stable and complete body, often interpret imagery such as the one-armed hero as a form of horrific dismemberment produced by violence. This article employs the concept of the “dividual” to capture the body's permeable, divisible, and reconfigurable nature, which is prominent in these works. From this perspective, the mutilation of the human body becomes an opportunity for relational and technological assemblages with nonhuman objects, as particularly exemplified in Chang Cheh's martial arts films and Ni Kuang's science fiction. Situated within a specific historical context shaped by the dynamics of war, industrialization, and postcolonialism, the representation of the fragmented body in 1960s and 1970s Hong Kong popular culture presents not only the dismembered body but also an attempt to negotiate and respond to it. This response involves constructing a dividual Hong Kong body that is constantly open to the process of heterogeneous assemblage and becoming.

  4. Deleuze and Guattari's Despotic and Capitalist Social Machines in Zhang Yimou's Raise the Red Lantern
    Richard Letteri · pp. 60-94
    摘要

    Chinese film scholars have long established the ways in which the Fifth Generation of filmmakers employ rural allegory to symbolize the patriarchal authoritarianism that has reigned over the Chinese people. However, focusing my analysis on Zhang Yimou's Raise the Red Lantern (Dahong denglong gaogao gua) from 1991, I suggest that to understand the struggles of Songlian, the film's female protagonist, it is necessary to address not only the repressive patriarchal forces of Confucian morality in combination with the authoritarian character of the Communist party-state but consider also the forces unleashed by liberalism and capitalism from the May Fourth Movement through Deng Xiaoping's policy of economic modernization. I develop my analysis of the film through Gilles Deleuze and Félix Guattari's understanding of the despotic and capitalist social machines. I show how these social machines release the forces of territorialization and deterritorialization in ways consistent with the polyvalent forces at play in twentieth-century China and explain how Songlian, who represents both the students who participated in the 1989 Democracy Movement and the Chinese people more generally, moves from a political state of paranoid neurosis to one of catatonic psychosis. This essay thus presents a “schizoanalytic” investigation of the conflicting forces of deterritorialization and reterritorialization and their effects on Chinese subjects caught between the despotic and capitalist social machines.

  5. The Rebellious Manchu Self in the Early Work of Liu Honglin (Xiao Jun)
    Martin Blahota · pp. 95-119
    摘要

    Liu Honglin, better known by his pen name Xiao Jun, is widely recognized as the author of one of the first novels to expose the situation in occupied Manchuria, Village in August (Bayue de xiangcun), whose publication in 1935 served to unite the Chinese nation in a time of crisis. However, a reading of Liu Honglin’s early self-referential work, published only a few years before the Japanese occupation, indicates that in the late 1920s, his vision of Manchuria’s attachment to China was highly complicated. This paper argues that the portrayal of the Manchu self in Liu’s stories and essays published mainly in 1929 strained the relationship with China represented in these writings. The paper places Liu Honglin’s early work in the context of the broader sociohistorical factors of the period to demonstrate that it narrates a fragmented borderland subjectivity informed by the marginal position of Manchus and Manchurian Chinese. Proposing to see Liu as an intellectual whose work challenged discrimination against minority groups within mainstream Han Chinese society, the paper opens up a space for a reassessment of Liu Honglin’s early work, highlighting a neglected and unexpected Manchu voice in the history of modern Chinese literature.

  6. Happy Together: The Socialist Utopia in a Shanghai Worker's Apartment
    Antonio Yijiao Guo · pp. 120-145
    摘要

    This paper analyzes the visual manifestation of happiness in the propaganda poster A Happy Life Given by Chairman Mao, designed by Xin Liliang and published in 1954 in Shanghai. Special attention is paid to how material abundance defines a utopian vision of socialism via the potlatch composition. The poster embodies a paradox: people can only understand socialism by celebrating and fetishizing an array of commodities such as foodstuffs, furniture, appliances, and the poster itself. At the same time, the symbolic contentment of materiality forges a “nostalgia” for a socialist utopia that is ambiguously defined in time and space. The poster consequently validates the policies regarding labor, production, and redistribution, which, in turn, are believed to bring about the envisioned contentment and happiness. Eventually, the individual pursuit of happiness is intertwined with a national metanarrative of the promised bright future, even though the tension between individuals and the state is ubiquitous and irreconcilable.

  7. Hsia Yü's Algorithmic Authorship: Reading Pink Noise as Computer-Generated Text
    Kate Costello · pp. 146-167
    摘要

    This article examines the work of Huang Qingqi (1956–), an enigmatic figure in the Taiwanese literary scene better known by her pen name Hsia Yü. Her 2007 landmark collection Pink Noise (Fenhongse zaoyin) is celebrated for its unique material form and creation process: translated pink lines on clear acetate pages, with text attributed to the Apple Sherlock software system. In laying claims to algorithmic authorship, Pink Noise is a computer-generated text, the product of an ambiguous collaboration between human and machine. Interrogating Hsia Yü's creative process, the paratextual proclamations about machine translation serve to obscure human agency and labor practices in the book's creation. The contested authorial status of the poetry demands attentiveness to textual detail. Reading Pink Noise as a computer-generated text in its full textual detail reveals unsettling grammatical slippages, its saccharine imagery veering toward the dystopian and the surreal. This article argues that Pink Noise merits a position alongside other works of algorithmic authorship; its fragile textuality deserves attentive reading and rereading.
